**Acceptance count: taught in 51 countries (Bangladesh,
Egypt, Ghana, China, Indonesia, Sierra Leone, Jordan, Greece, Kazakhstan, Iraq, Ethiopia, South Africa, Syria, South Sudan, Nepal, Armenia, Vietnam, Cambodia, Pakistan, Sudan, Kuwait, Rwanda, Malaysia, Yemen, Afghanistan, Ecuador, Saudi Arabia, Uzbekistan, Venezuela, Tanzania, Albania, Bolivia, Mexico, India, Mongolia, Nicaragua, North Macedonia, Palestine, Laos, Libya, Cyprus, Fiji, Eritrea, Tunisia, Samoa, Uganda, Uruguay, Sri Lanka, Suriname, Liechtenstein, Bhutan).** Liechtenstein joins with the five story picture-dictionary blurbs of its LinGo 4 books — plot summaries of the blurbs only; the story books themselves are not in hand (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Liechtenstein expansion merge). Suriname joins with the Reader for Grade 10 rows stocked below (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Suriname expansion merge). Sri Lanka joins with the Sri Lanka rows stocked below (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Sri Lanka expansion merge). Uruguay joins with the #LivingUruguay rows stocked in this file (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Uruguay expansion merge). Cyprus joins with the programme's story spine — one mediated picturebook per unit across Years 1–6, from 'I Want My Dinner' to 'Eat Your Peas' (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Cyprus expansion merge). Libya joins with writing a story — guided story composition (Grade 8) (stocked 2026-11-11 by the re-verification sweep merge). North Macedonia joins with its Grade 2 course's eight module stories — the birthday story read in the pupil book's secured opening, the rest as Teacher's Book evidence, all as plot summaries (stocked 2026-11-11 by the re-verification sweep merge). India joins with stories from all twelve of its NCERT books — the primary folk tales and picture stories of Marigold, the Honeysuckle, Honeycomb and Honeydew stories, Beehive's and First Flight's named stories, and Hornbill's and Flamingo's long prose (stocked 2026-11-09 by the India re-verification sweep merge). Ghana names the narrative mode; Bangladesh
teaches stories from its earliest classes with the fables;
Egypt reads whole stories through the secondary years;
China names the story's three parts outright (setting,
characters, plot) and binds complete play scripts to
perform; the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep also
stocked China's 43 Story time tales and Grade 9
story-writing tasks — every primary PEP book closes each
unit with a Zoom-and-Zip tale, all previously unstocked. Indonesia tables the narrative's own stages
(Orientation, Complication, Resolution — and a Coda at
Grade 9) on the stories themselves and re-teaches the
definition whole; Sierra Leone names characters, setting
and plot from Primary 4–5 and restates the shared elements
(plot, theme, characters, setting, conflict, point of
view) in the SSS3 creative-writing lesson; Jordan reads
a model detective story and writes one at Grade 7 under
its "How to write… a good story" reference, sets a short
story as a Grade 10 writing outcome with its linkers
grouped, and writes a story inside Grade 11's
narrative-tenses unit; Greece gives a whole primary unit
to stories — a fairy tale reconstructed and dramatised,
then the pupil's own short story — names a Grade 7 lesson
"Tell me a story!" over a prize-winning model, and teaches
narrative craft outright at Grades 11–12, where the story
is charted and enriched with background, flashback and
monologue; Kazakhstan gives whole modules to stories —
its Grade 6 book glosses the story's own elements (plot,
character, setting, conflict) beside Oliver Twist and
sets a story as the module's writing outcome, its
Grade 7 book closes each story text with a theory box
telling narrative, legends and folk tales apart, and
its other Grade 6 course prints story-writing advice
beside its model story; Iraq prints stories throughout
its primary course — six two-page picture stories bound
as their own section at Grade 1, a story closing every
main unit at Grade 2 (The toy maker, Goldilocks and the
three bears, The rabbit and the tortoise among them),
and six stories with printed text at Grade 3, the later
ones in full past-tense narration;
Ethiopia stocks the story in part — narrative writing
is taught as the narrative essay at Grades 11 and 12,
and stories are read whole from primary school up
(the Grade 3 fable "The Running Race", the Grade 2
story "Birra and Bari", the Grade 9 "Spooky Story"
carrying its past-tense work) — but no read Ethiopia
book names the story's furniture (setting, characters,
plot) as such, so that naming rests on the other
countries; South Africa names the story's furniture outright — its Grade 5 Home Language Workbook 2 analyses stories by character, plot and setting (printed pp.36–39) and closes with a fiction summit defining the fable, folk tale, myth, short story, novel and drama (printed pp.128–129), its Grade 6 First Additional Language Workbook 2 analyses story elements (worksheet 83, printed p.58), and its Grade 4 Home Language Workbook 1 writes stories from a mind map (Theme 1) and defines the fable (Theme 4); Syria stocks the story in part — complete stories are read from the first book up (Bumbo and Little Red Riding Hood at Grade 1, the farmer and the well and the tortoise and the rabbit at Grade 2, Samir and the Magic Paintbrush at Grade 3, module stories at Grade 9, and a whole prose retelling of the Epic of Gilgamesh at Grade 12) — but no read Syria book names the story's furniture (setting, characters, plot) as a taught craft; plot appears only as a literary term to learn in the Grade 12 Epics unit, so that naming rests on the other countries; South Sudan joined 2026-11-03: it names the story's furniture outright and teaches it whole — its Primary 5 book's story unit teaches the elements of a story by name (setting, character, plot, conflict, resolution, theme, printed p.153), its Primary 6 book gives a whole unit to short stories (vocabulary matched to definitions, elements identified, openers and endings, conjunctions spotted in stories), its Primary 7 literature course works its prose extracts for setting, characters and themes, and its Primary 8 book revisits story elements, names story styles (suspense, coincidence, flashback, letters) and teaches play conventions. Nepal joined 2026-11-03 in part: stories are read whole through the set and story writing is worked from Grade 7 — reordering, writing from hints and points, skeleton-story writing, story ordering and a story from an opening line — with seven complete literature stories at Grade 11, but no read Nepal book names the story's furniture (setting, characters, plot) as a taught craft, so that naming rests on the other countries. Kuwait names the story's furniture in Pearls 6A:
its story-elements table — characters, setting, problem,
solution, moral — is completed for The Honest Woodcutter,
and its favourite-story organiser works character, events
in first/then/finally order and lesson on a model paragraph
about Alice in Wonderland before the pupil's own story. Rwanda stocks the story in part — complete stories are read whole from P1 up, stories and fables are named topics in P2 and P5, Telling Stories is a named topic in P4, and story writing is set work through the set (narrations and alternative endings in P4, plan–draft–revise story work in P5, set-title stories in P6) — but no read Rwanda book names the story's furniture (setting, characters, plot) as a taught set, so that naming rests on the other countries. Tanzania stocks the story in part — one whole Standard One reading book of complete short stories (The wrong fish, The foolish rats, The magic tree, The Hen and the Kite among them), its Introduction naming story-telling as the course's own aim — but no read Tanzania book names the story's furniture (setting, characters, plot) as such, so that naming rests on the other countries. Other closed countries join as their books are read (see
READING-QUEUE.md). Mexico stocks story at project scale: its Grade 7 book's literary festival and the complete tale “The Kindness Princess”, and its Grade 8 book's La Llorona artistic proposal (stocked 2026-11-09 by the Mexico re-verification sweep merge). Mongolia joins with English XI and English XII, its Grade 11 and Grade 12 books, read page by page for the first time in the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Mongolia re-verification sweep merge). Eritrea joins with the *English for Eritrea* books (Grades 1–12) stocked below (stocked 2026-11-11 by the Eritrea expansion merge).

**Barbados stocked 2026-11-11 (re-verification sweep merge):** Barbados's rows in this lesson are curriculum evidence only — Barbados's set holds no pupil textbook (its held set is the four Primary Language Arts Syllabi, Classes 1–4), so the taught-in acceptance count above does not change.

**Ghana — further evidence stocked 2026-11-10 (re-verification sweep merge):** the sweep read Ghana's full Distance Learning set (six teacher guides and three learners' workbooks) and the NaCCA curriculum standards not previously cited; the new rows are teacher, curriculum and workbook evidence only — Ghana's set holds no pupil textbook, so the taught-in acceptance count above does not change.

**South Africa added 2026-11-03** — three books stock this lesson: *English Home Language, Grade 4 Workbook 1*, *English Home Language, Grade 5 Workbook 2* and *English First Additional Language, Grade 6 Workbook 2* (Rainbow Workbooks) — South Africa, Department of Basic Education, Sixteenth edition 2026; full read in english/books/south-africa.md. The Home Language Grade 5 Workbook 2 analyses stories by character, plot and setting (printed pp.36–39) and ends with a fiction summit defining the fable, folk tale, myth, short story, novel and drama (printed pp.128–129); the First Additional Language Grade 6 Workbook 2 analyses story elements (worksheet 83, printed p.58); and the Home Language Grade 4 Workbook 1 writes stories from a mind map (Theme 1) and defines the fable (Theme 4).

**Syria added 2026-11-03** — PARTIAL stock, filed exactly as a partial: five books stock this lesson in part: *Emar Pupil's Book 1*, *Emar Pupil's Book 2*, *Emar Pupil's Book 3*, *Emar Student's Book 9* and *Emar English Series 12, Student's Book, Literary Section* — Syria, Ministry of Education; full read in english/books/syria.md. Complete stories are read from the first book up: Bumbo and Little Red Riding Hood close Grade 1 (Stories, pp.83–94); Grade 2's Story Time holds the farmer and the well and the tortoise and the rabbit (pp.83–92); Grade 3's Stories from Around the World holds Samir and the Birds and The Magic Paintbrush, closed by an event-ordering exercise (pp.78–86); Grade 9's modules carry The Happy Fisherman (pp.26–27), the flying-turtle fable (pp.54–55) and Three Wishes (p.78); and Grade 12 reads a whole prose retelling of the Epic of Gilgamesh (Unit 11, pp.108–115). Stated plainly: no read Syria book names the story's furniture — setting, characters, plot — as a taught craft; plot appears only among the literary terms (novel, plot, satire, tragedy, masterpiece) in that Grade 12 unit, and the Grade 12 literary book otherwise teaches the history and terms of each genre and prints no complete literary text.

**Syria — *English for Starters 5* added 2026-11-06** — 1 further Syria book stocks this lesson: **Book:** *English for Starters 5*, Pupil's Book (pupil's book) — Syria, York Press, new edition 2008, reprinted 2014, no ISBN printed. Filed in english/books/syria.md (Grade 5 — English for Starters 5, Pupil's Book (York Press) section, FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-06). A sixth Syria book stocks this lesson: the older York Press Grade 5 pupil's book prints a complete graded story in full at its back — "Snow White and the Seven Dwarfs" (printed pp.79–87), an abridged retelling for learners with a multiple-choice check for every story page (printed p.88). The plot only is filed here; no story text is carried into the lesson. The same reader is also stocked in story/the-big-reading, on the bound-in graded-reader precedents filed there.

**South Sudan added 2026-11-03** — four books stock this lesson: **Book:** *Primary English 5 Pupil's Book* — South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction, © 2018; **Book:** *Primary English 6 Pupil's Book* — South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction, © 2018; **Book:** *Primary English 7 Pupil's Book* — South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction, © 2018; **Book:** *Primary English 8 Pupil's Book* — South Sudan, Ministry of General Education and Instruction, © 2018. All filed in english/books/south-sudan.md (Primary 5 — Primary English 5 Pupil's Book, Primary 6 — Primary English 6 Pupil's Book, Primary 7 — Primary English 7 Pupil's Book and Primary 8 — Primary English 8 Pupil's Book sections, FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03). The Primary 5 book's closing unit, Writing a story, teaches the elements of a story by name — setting, character, plot, conflict, resolution, theme (printed p.153) — on its model story "SURPRISE!" (printed pp.150–151), then has pupils plan and write a picture-based story and learn summarising (printed p.155); the Primary 6 book's Short Stories unit matches story vocabulary (plot, setting, characters, theme, conflict, narrator) to definitions, identifies elements of a short story and works story openers and endings (Unit 7, printed pp.88–100); the Primary 7 book's literature units read prose extracts — Ngugi, Achebe and Paton in Unit 9, Narayan and Updike in Unit 10 — with comprehension on setting, characters and themes for every extract; and the Primary 8 book revisits story elements and names story styles (suspense, coincidence, flashback, stories told through letters) around its complete short story "Without a Trace" (Unit 5, printed pp.62–74), then teaches play conventions and play writing (Unit 8, printed pp.99–109).

**Nepal added 2026-11-03** — PARTIAL stock, filed exactly as a partial: three books stock this lesson in part: **Book:** *English Grade 7* — Nepal, Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, Curriculum Development Centre. **Book:** *English Grade 8* — Nepal, Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, Curriculum Development Centre. **Book:** *English (Grade 11)* — Nepal, Government of Nepal, Ministry of Education, Science and Technology, Curriculum Development Centre. All filed in english/books/nepal.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03). Story writing is worked from the story's own order: Grade 7 **reorders "The Hare and the Tortoise" and writes the thirsty-crow story from hints** (Unit 13, printed pp.128–141), **reorders and develops stories** (the lion and the mouse; the bundle of sticks, Unit 16, printed pp.166–178) and **writes the crane-and-fish story from points** (Unit 8, printed pp.77–87); Grade 8 writes **skeleton stories** (Unit 2, printed pp.13–24), practises **story ordering and picture-story writing** (Unit 16, printed pp.201–213) and writes **a story from an opening line** (Unit 19, printed pp.238–253); and Grade 11 reads **seven complete literature stories whole**, each with its study apparatus (Section Two, printed pp.193–240). Stated plainly: no read Nepal book names the story's furniture — setting, characters, plot — as a taught craft, so that naming rests on the other countries.

**Armenia added 2026-11-03** — two books stock this lesson: **Book:** *English 9* — Armenia, COAF, Yerevan 2025, no ISBN printed. **Book:** *English 7* — Armenia, Zangak, Yerevan 2023, Grigoryan, ISBN 978-9939-99-035-4. All filed in english/books/armenia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03). **Vietnam added 2026-11-03** — PARTIAL stock, filed exactly as a partial: 1 book stocks this lesson in part: **Book:** *Tiếng Anh 5, Volume 2 (Global Success)* — Vietnam, Vietnam Education Publishing House & Macmillan, © 2024, ISBN 978-604-0-39362-3. All filed in english/books/vietnam.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03). Grade 5, Volume 2's Stories for children unit (printed pp.46–51) reads one complete short story whole — "The Fox and the Crow" — and names the story's characters as its element: "Who are the main characters in the story?", with a Character Map as the writing work and a Telling a story project. Stated plainly: setting and plot are never named as taught craft anywhere in the sixteen books, so Vietnam's stock here is the characters and the whole-story reading, and the rest of the story's furniture rests on the other countries.

**Cambodia added 2026-11-03** — 2 books stock this lesson: **Book:** *English Grade 6* (student's book) — Cambodia, MoEYS, 5th printing 2020, ISBN 978-9924-9423-1-3. **Book:** *ENGLISH Grade 9* (student's book) — Cambodia, MoEYS with The Nippon Foundation / Education Support Center "KIZUNA", first printing 2016, no ISBN printed. All filed in english/books/cambodia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03). Cambodia names the story's parts outright — a step beyond most of the pool: Grade 9's creative-writing unit lists the ingredients of a good story as character, setting, plot, problem and moral, then has pupils write, edit and rewrite a story of their own (Unit 18); Grade 6's closing chapter is six folk tales, each ending on its moral (Chapter 6).

**Pakistan added 2026-11-03** — 4 books stock this lesson: **Book:** *English 4* (student's book) — Pakistan, Punjab Curriculum & Textbook Board (PCTB), Lahore, Single National Curriculum 2020, no ISBN printed. **Book:** *English 5* (student's book) — Pakistan, Punjab Curriculum & Textbook Board (PCTB), Lahore, the board's older-format reader; no SNC line printed, no ISBN printed. **Book:** *English 9* (student's book) — Pakistan, Punjab Curriculum & Textbook Board (PCTB), Lahore, Updated/Revised National Curriculum 2023, Experimental Edition, no ISBN printed. **Book:** *English Book I* (student's book) — Pakistan, Punjab Curriculum & Textbook Board (PCTB), Lahore, intermediate literature book (15 short stories), April 2018 printing, no ISBN printed. All filed in english/books/pakistan.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-03). Pakistan names the story's furniture outright, early and late: Grade 4 defines each element of a story — characters, setting, beginning, middle, conflict, ending — and builds the pupil's own story on a story web (Unit 10, printed p.97). Grade 5 teaches story structure — beginning, middle, end — with a story sheet of title, main characters, problem and solution (Text 12, printed pp.87–88), and Grade 9 names the elements of a story — setting, characters, plot, conflict, resolution, theme — as its story-writing work (Unit 6, printed pp.81–82). Book I is fifteen complete short stories and nothing else, each closed by a printed Theme paragraph (printed pp.1–100).

**Sudan added 2026-11-04** — PARTIAL stock, filed exactly as a partial: 4 books stock this lesson in part: **Book:** *SMILE Series: Book 6 — Pupil's Book, Grade 8: Basic Level* (pupil's book) — Sudan, The National Centre for Curricula and Educational Research (NCCER), November 2016 (the introduction's date; no edition statement is printed), no ISBN printed. **Book:** *SMILE Series: Book 9 — Pupil's Book, Grade: intermidate Level 3* [sic] (pupil's book) — Sudan, The National Centre for Curricula and Educational Research (NCCER), no year, edition or ISBN printed. **Book:** *THE SPINE SERIES / PUPIL'S BOOK 5 — SPINE 5* (pupil's book) — Sudan, Ministry of Education, The Sudan / National Center for Curriculum Development and Educational Research, first published 1996, ISBN 978-99942-919-9-6. **Book:** *THE SPINE SERIES / PUPIL'S BOOK 6 — SPINE 6* (pupil's book) — Sudan, Ministry of Education, the Sudan / National Centre for Curriculum Development and Educational Research, first published 1997, ISBN 978-99942-919-8-9. Complete stories are read whole across the set: the Grade 8 book's folk tales (Juha's Nail, printed p.25; the Princess and the Ring, printed p.2), its Oliver Twist summary and adapted extract (printed pp.30–31) and its adapted *Little Women* passage (printed p.46); the Grade 9 book's "The Wise Fox" (printed p.9), simplified *Robinson Crusoe* (printed p.13) and closing Juha story (printed p.64); and SPINE 5's full extract from Tayeb Salih's "The Wedding of Zein" (Chapter Nine, printed pp.125–138). Sudan also names two pieces of story shape: SPINE 5's narrative lesson identifies a story's scene, action and consequences in a wrongly-ordered football-crowd story before the pupil writes a three-paragraph story (Chapter Twelve), and SPINE 6's narrative rule box names the shape — "good narratives have: a beginning, a middle and an end!" — before a 300–400-word narrative (Chapter Eight). Stated plainly: no read Sudan book names the story's furniture — setting, characters, plot — as a taught set, so that naming rests on the other countries.

**Kuwait added 2026-11-04** — English Pearls of Kuwait Student's Book 6A (full read in english/books/kuwait.md). Kuwait names the story's furniture outright in one book: Pearls 6A completes a story-elements table — characters, setting, problem, solution, moral — for its tale The Honest Woodcutter (Unit 4, Lesson 5, printed pp.67–68). The same unit's writing lesson works a favourite-story organiser — character, events in first/then/finally order, lesson — on a model paragraph about Alice in Wonderland, then turns it to the pupil's own favourite story (Unit 4, Lesson 7, printed pp.70–71).

**Malaysia added 2026-11-04** — 3 books stock this lesson: **Book:** Form 5 KPM textbook under licence from Hamilton House Publishers — Malaysia, Malaysian ISBN 978-967-0951-44-7, Malaysian edition first published 2021; the copy is front-incomplete — printed pp.1–4 are absent and no title is printed in the copy, so only held pages are cited. **Book:** *Close-Up* B1 Student's Book, Second Edition — Malaysia (generic international edition), National Geographic Learning / Cengage © 2016, ISBN 978-1-4080-9554-6. **Book:** *Full Blast! Malaysia stocks the story at three levels: Form 5 Unit 2's Writing (pp.26–27) teaches making stories lifelike through direct speech, thoughts and feelings, with a model story analysed by paragraph; Form 3 Unit 4's Writing (pp.43–54) teaches writing a story — thinking of ideas, organising a story, describing people; and Form 4 Module 8's Let's Read 4 (pp.124–125) teaches the terms foreshadowing, cliffhanger and direct speech on a survival-story extract. The Form 3 limit stands as filed: *Close-Up* B1 is the generic international edition, with no Malaysian or KPM imprint in the copy.

Book 6 (Grade 6) adds five complete stories: Sukkar and the crocodiles (Unit 1, Lessons 5–6, printed pp.5–6), the stone-soup traveller (Unit 3, Lessons 5–6, printed pp.21–22), the wren and the falcon (Unit 5, Lesson 6, printed p.38), the fox and the crow (Unit 7, Lesson 5, printed p.53), and Murad, the boy who cried illness — feigning sickness four times, then truly ill, not believed, and dead in the night (Unit 6, Lesson 5, printed p.45); that ending is filed exactly as the book prints it, not softened. All are plot stock only, on the same terms as the Sudan stories above.

**Malaysia — primary pupil's books added 2026-11-06** — 4 further Malaysia books stock this lesson, read directly: **Book:** *Super Minds Student's Book 1* (pupil's book; one volume serving Year 1, printed pp.4–57, and Year 2, printed pp.58–117) — Malaysia, Cambridge University Press, first published 2012 (this copy 15th printing 2015), ISBN 978-0-521-14855-9. **Book:** *Get Smart Plus 3* Student's Book (pupil's book, Year 3) — Malaysia, MM Publications with Syarikat Arena Ilmu, no year printed in the copy, ISBN 978-983-805-039-5 (the held copy is defective — printed pp.60–61 and p.113 are missing — and no row in this lesson rests on those pages). **Book:** *Get Smart Plus 4* Student's Book (pupil's book, Year 4) — Malaysia, MM Publications, Malaysian edition first published 2019, ISBN 978-967-0460-38-3. **Book:** *Academy Stars* Pupil's Book, Year 6 (pupil's book) — Malaysia, Macmillan Education / Institut Terjemahan & Buku Malaysia for the Ministry of Education Malaysia, first published 2021, ISBN 978-967-460-874-3. All filed in english/books/malaysia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-06 — 130 anchors tested across the five books, 129 passed exactly as cited, 1 corrected in place, 0 not found). Malaysia was already stocked in this lesson, so its countries line and acceptance count do not move; what changes is the evidence — primary stock previously carried as Scheme of Work / mapped coverage (or held only in the secondary books) is now stocked from the pupil's books themselves. Direct stock now read in the pupil's books — *Super Minds* 1, Year 1: first picture story: the four friends meet and show their powers; a cat answers with its name (plot only) (pp.8–9); go-kart race story: a cheating rival is beaten and fair play wins (plot only) (pp.26–27); spider story: children who fear a spider come to like spiders (plot only) (pp.38–39); pizza story: a girl jumps the lunch queue, is sent back, and fresh pizza arrives for everyone (plot only) (pp.50–51); *Super Minds* 1, Year 2: lost-in-the-woods story: the friends follow a rabbit to the lake, then one boy is lost himself (plot only) (pp.62–63); old-house story: an invisible friend explores a spooky house room by room while the others wait outside (plot only) (pp.74–75); cap story: a boy accuses a classmate of taking his cap; the caps are identical, a bird returns the real one, and he apologises (plot only) (pp.86–87); robot story: the friends build a robot that speaks backwards until one of them fixes it (plot only) (pp.98–99); hill-race story: a rock blocks the race route; the friends move it together and walk up together instead of racing (plot only) (pp.110–111); Get Smart Plus 3: Jerry's painting story: a painted dog is revealed as the real dog sitting for its portrait; the trick ending carries the value "don't play tricks" (plot only) (pp.12–13); Career Day story: visiting parents describe their jobs and the class goldfish "wants to be a cat" (plot only) (pp.22–23); table-tennis story: Tom fetches what he thinks is the lost ball and Nancy hits an egg by mistake (plot only) (pp.32–33); snow-globe story: children are pulled into a snow globe, play in the snow, and land back on the beach (plot only) (pp.42–43); ghost story: children hunt a "ghost" through the house and find only their own mess to tidy (plot only) (pp.52–53); cooking-competition story: Fay practises cooking with her father for a month and wins the class competition (plot only) (pp.62–63); secret-tunnel story: children follow a map through a tunnel under the park and come out in Grandma's garden (plot only) (pp.72–73); bear-cub story: children find an injured cub, the vet treats it, and they return it to its mother (plot only) (pp.92–93); Papigo story: a poor farmer wins the princess's thanks by being the fastest when her horse bolts (plot only) (pp.102–103); Get Smart Plus 4: museum comic: on a school trip the children meet new friends from other countries and a mummy seems to come alive (plot only) (p.6); Academy Stars Year 6: "A trip to remember": Abi pedals her grandmother's old bike forwards into a future of skyscrapers and polluted air, then pedals backwards home valuing fresh air (plot only) (pp.60–61); The Perfect Swan: the emperor waits three years for a painting the artist paints in two minutes — after three years of practice (plot only) (pp.96–97); The robbery: a play script in which Inspector Fox questions six suspects about a stolen dinosaur egg (plot only) (pp.122–123).

**Albania added 2026-11-08 (re-verification sweep)** — 1 book stocks this lesson: **Book:** *Akelius A2 Level — A Practical Guide for English Language Teachers* (teacher's guide) — Albania, UNICEF Albania / ASCAP, 2025; no ISBN printed. Filed in english/books/albania.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED 2026-11-04). Filed plainly as teacher-guide evidence: the guide's health chapter (Chapter 35, "Going to the doctor") carries one named platform reading, "Tommy's accident" (printed p.34) — a boy hurt playing football behind his house, his father calling the emergency services. The guide describes the reading; the story's text is not printed in the guide, so the stock is the plot exactly as the guide gives it, and no word of the story itself is cited.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's held English set from the PDFs — 46 in-hand files holding 42 distinct books (1,469 PDF pages): the Ministry of Education and Culture 'Friends' Years 1–4 and 'The Magic Box' Years 5–6 primary programme. Two SHA-256 byte-identical duplicate pairs were collapsed (the Year 5 Unit 8/9 pupil and teacher files), and three Year 6 Activity Book unit files are exact page excerpts of the whole Activity Book and were read in it. 261 teaching items were placed fresh from the books; Cyprus had no presence in the course before, so all 261 were missing. The rows below are this lesson's share, stocked from the expansion placements. Evidence kinds are labelled as what they are: Years 1–4 are held as Teacher's Books (Year 1 is an oral-only year — no pupil's book exists for it), the Year 5–6 teacher books are per-unit files, and rows resting on a Pupil's Book or the Activity Book say so in their book entry. Read depth, as the placements declare it: full page-by-page reads of the pupil, activity and project files and of every teacher-book unit header, planning map, story/reading section and vocabulary list; teacher-book lesson-procedure prose is delivery of the header items and is not stocked separately. Stories and readings are stocked as plot summaries only.

## Fiji added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Fiji's held English set from the PDFs — 13 in-hand files holding 12 distinct titles (the Year 11 draft is an earlier version of the Year 11 Communications book, not a separate title): one student's book per grade for Years 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12 and 13 — no Year 7 English book exists in the set, and the set holds no teacher books, so every row below rests on a student's book. 335 teaching items were placed fresh from the books; Fiji had no presence in the course before, so all 335 were missing. The rows below are this lesson's share, stocked from the expansion placements. Read depth, as the placements declare it: Years 1, 3, 4, 5 and 9 were read in full, page by page (Year 2, an image-only scan, in full from page renders); Years 6, 8, 10, 11 (both files), 12 and 13 were read at full unit level — every unit's texts, comprehension work, grammar/language focus, writing focus and outcomes — but not every exercise line on every page; that limit is carried here honestly, and a later citation-check pass owes those books line-level depth. Stories and readings are stocked as plot summaries only.
Book errors, carried: the Year 4 book's Contents lists its Unit 11 as "The Fox and the Stock" — the unit itself is the fable "The Fox and the Crow", and the row below cites the unit itself. In the Year 5 book, Unit 3's adapted fable renames the kitten Skippy, but the unit's own comprehension questions still name Rhonda, the kitten of the original story the unit adapts; the row below cites the adapted story as printed.
**Book 1:** *Language, Literacy and Communication* — Year 2 (student's book) — Fiji, Ministry of Education, Culture and Arts, Curriculum Development Unit (CDU) 2013, 64 pp. Image-only scan: read in full, page by page, from 200-dpi page renders (OCR used only as a finding aid). Printed page = PDF page. Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

## Tunisia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Tunisia's held English set from the PDFs — 11 distinct books (18 PDF files; three books are multi-part at source), all from the Ministry of Education's Centre National Pédagogique (CNP): Prime English Year 6; Let's Learn English Year 7 Student's and Activity Books; Let's Discover More English Year 8 Student's and Activity Books; Proceed with English Year 9 Student's and Activity Books; and the four secondary books Perform through English (Year 1), Perform to Learn (Year 2), Activate And Perform (Year 3) and Skills for Life (Year 4). 249 teaching items were placed fresh from the books; Tunisia had no presence in the course before, so all 249 were missing. The rows below are this lesson's share, stocked from the expansion placements. No teacher book is recorded for Tunisia English in any record checked — every row rests on a student's/pupil's book or an activity book, labelled as such in its book entry. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the seven basic-education books were read in full, page by page, through the PDFs' own text layers; the four secondary books were read through their complete book maps / unit outlines / contents, their grammar summaries and reference appendices in full, and page-by-page text scans of every lesson opening — and Activate And Perform's placements are stated at module level from its Book Map (pp.4–6) plus its Grammar Summary rather than at individual-lesson level. The books were taken from their recorded Internet Archive sources, byte-size verified against the records (flag F4: the Year 7 pair's in-hand status is inconsistent across the library records — reported in the CURRICULUM merge paragraph; the books themselves are read and placed either way). Stories and readings are stocked as plot summaries only.

**Book 1:** *Let's Learn English — 7th Year Basic Education, Activity Book* (activity book) — Tunisia, Ministry of Education, Centre National Pédagogique (CNP). Activity-book evidence — practice of the Student's Book modules, with its own stories placed separately; citations are printed pages. Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 2:** *Let's Discover More English — 8th Year Basic Education, Student's Book* (student's book) — Tunisia, Ministry of Education, Centre National Pédagogique (CNP). Student's-book evidence; held in two part files, both read; printed page = PDF page within each part's continuous pagination. Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 3:** *Proceed with English — 9th Year Basic Education, Student's Book* (student's book) — Tunisia, Ministry of Education, Centre National Pédagogique (CNP). Student's-book evidence; printed page = PDF page. Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 4:** *Proceed with English — 9th Year Basic Education, Activity Book* (activity book) — Tunisia, Ministry of Education, Centre National Pédagogique (CNP). Activity-book evidence; printed page = PDF page. Its own new content is the six Grandma's Corner readings, stocked as plot summaries only. Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 5:** *Perform through English — First Year Secondary Education, Pupil's Book* (pupil's book) — Tunisia, Ministry of Education, Centre National Pédagogique (CNP). Pupil's-book evidence; printed page ≈ PDF page. The library record files this secondary book under a grade-07 folder (flag F3, record-side, reported in the CURRICULUM merge paragraph). Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 6:** *Perform to Learn — Second Year Secondary Education, Student's Book* (student's book) — Tunisia, Ministry of Education, Centre National Pédagogique (CNP). Student's-book evidence; printed page = PDF page. The records title this book 'Perform through English — Second Year'; its own cover reads 'Perform to Learn' (flag F1, record-side). Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 7:** *Skills for Life — Year 4 Secondary Education, Students' Book* (students' book) — Tunisia, Ministry of Education, Centre National Pédagogique (CNP). Students'-book evidence; printed page = PDF page. The records file this one book twice (at grade-10 and grade-13) and misdescribe it in the download list (flag F2, record-side); it is cited once here, as the Year 4 book it is. Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

Book error E7, carried: Perform to Learn's contents/book map title for Lesson 14 is 'My boss paid me to lie' while the lesson's own heading in the body is 'Why I Had to Leave My Job' — the same lesson under two titles; the secretary-story row rests on that lesson.

## Samoa added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sāmoa's held English set from the PDFs — 11 books: ten student's books (Years 9–13) plus the *English Years 1–8 Primary School Curriculum* statement — every file byte-matched to its record before reading. 416 teaching items were placed fresh from the books; Sāmoa had no presence in the course before, so all 416 were missing. The rows below are this lesson's share, stocked from the expansion placements. Read depth, as the placements declare it and as every Sāmoa section states: the curriculum statement and the Year 9–11 books were read page by page in full from full text extraction; the two senior books (Year 12, 174 pp.; Year 13, 242 pp.) were read at unit depth — every unit introduction, activity heading, language and grammar section and model text read, every comprehension passage identified from its questions and framing text, and the long reprinted literary texts inside them read for their teaching apparatus rather than line by line as literature. Book A is a curriculum statement, not a pupil textbook: its rows rest on the statement's attainment targets and are cited as curriculum evidence of that kind; every other row rests on a student's book. In Books B–K the printed page = PDF page − 1; in Book A the printed page = the PDF page. Stories and readings are stocked as plot summaries only.

**Book 1:** *English Years 1–8 — Primary School Curriculum* (curriculum statement) — Sāmoa, Curriculum Materials and Assessment Division (CMAD), Ministry of Education, Sports and Culture, January 2013; ISBN 978-982-517-091-4; written by Anne Leauga; 90 pp. A curriculum statement, not a pupil textbook: the rows resting on it cite its attainment targets and are curriculum evidence of that kind. Printed page = PDF page. Years 1–2 carry Listening & Speaking / Interpersonal pages only; the three-strand structure begins at Year 3 (bilingual policy). ## Uganda added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Uganda's in-hand English set from the PDFs: five NCDC *Home-Study Learning* English booklets — *English Home-Study Learning, Primary Five* and *English Language Home-Study Learning, Senior One–Four* (Ministry of Education and Sports, August 2020), all student's books, read page by page — plus the English learning area of the *National Primary School Curriculum for Uganda, Teacher's Guide, Primary Three* (NCDC, 2008), which is teacher evidence only. 99 teaching items were placed fresh from the books; Uganda had no presence in the course before, so all 99 were missing. A seventh in-hand file, the *Primary Five Home-Study Merged Package* (NCDC, 2020), reprints the Primary Five booklet's English section lesson for lesson (all 27 lesson headings verified; pages spot-compared identical in content and printed pagination) and is collapsed into that booklet — no separate rows rest on it. The rows below are this lesson's share, stocked from the expansion placements (liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-2026-11-10/english/uganda-placements.md). Stories and readings are stocked as plot summaries only. Printed page = PDF page − 6 in the five booklets.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Sri Lanka's in-hand English set from the PDFs: **25 distinct documents** (31 record files; 6 byte-identical duplicate files collapsed with hash proof — the Grade 8 Teacher's Guide 2017 filed under three language names is one English-medium guide, the Grade 12 and Grade 13 A/L English and General English guides are single two-grade guides filed twice each, the Grade 7 English Language Guide 2007 'Tamil' file is the English file, and the ABOE 2018 Key Stage 1 handbook is one book for Grades 1 and 2 filed under both grades). The union set is: 3 EPD pupil's books (Grades 6, 8, 9), 4 EPD workbooks (Grades 6–9), 17 NIE teacher guides and manuals, the 2 old ABOE teacher guides plus the ABOE 2018 Key Stage 1 handbook, and the O/L *Appreciation of English Literary Texts* syllabus bound with its Teacher's Resource Book. No Grade 7 pupil's book exists in any record — Grade 7 rests on its workbook and teacher guides (flag F2). 250 teaching items were placed fresh from the books; Sri Lanka had no presence in the course before, so all 250 were missing. Evidence kinds are labelled in every book entry below: pupil's-book and workbook rows rest on pupil-side books; teacher-guide rows are teacher-guide evidence of the taught syllabus (the taught content of the NIE guides IS their syllabus/competency tables). Read depth, as the placements declare it: full page-by-page text-layer reads of the ABOE guides and handbook, the Grade 3, 5, 7 (2007 syllabus), 9, A/L and Appreciation books; the NIE secondary guides (Grades 6, 7 (2016), 8, 10, 11) read through their complete syllabus/competency tables plus the guide bodies' competency chapters; the EPD pupil books and workbooks read at full unit level with grammar sections verified line-level — except the Grade 6 Workbook, an image-only scan read from rendered pages (flag F9). The placements' named exclusions (front matter and boilerplate, assessment and pedagogy apparatus, the ABOE handbook's Sinhala-medium sections) stand as declared and are stocked nowhere. Stories and prescribed literary texts are stocked as titles and plot summaries only. The rows below are this lesson's share, stocked from the expansion placements.

**Book 1:** *Activity Based Oral English*, Grade 2 — teacher guide (old edition) — Sri Lanka. The same 16 ERA themes as Grade 1 at Grade 2 depth; teacher-guide evidence. Printed page = PDF page. Read in full in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 2:** *English Language Teacher's Guide*, Grade 6 — National Institute of Education (NIE), First Edition 2014 (syllabus for 2015) — Sri Lanka. NIE syllabus (47 competency levels) plus guide; teacher-guide evidence. Its syllabus table is printed pp.15–42. Read in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 3:** *English Language Teacher's Guide*, Grade 7 — National Institute of Education (NIE), 2007 syllabus edition — Sri Lanka. Syllabus plus fully worked lesson sequences carrying the actual pupil texts and dialogues; teacher-guide evidence, a different edition from the 2016 Grade 7 guide. (The record's 'Tamil' file is this same English file — one blob, cited once; flag F5.) Read in full in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 4:** *English Workbook*, Grade 7 — Educational Publications Department (EPD), First Print 2015 — Sri Lanka. A pupil workbook; no Grade 7 pupil's textbook is in hand in any record (flag F2), so this workbook — whose units mirror the textbook's — is the pupil-side evidence for Grade 7. Read at full unit level in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 5:** *English Grade 9 Teacher's Instruction Manual* — National Institute of Education (NIE), First Print 2009 — Sri Lanka. The 2009-syllabus manual, a different edition from the 2018 Grade 9 guide; teacher-guide evidence. Book error carried: its p.11 prints a production placeholder ('insert the syllabus') where the syllabus table should stand, so its competency levels are cited only where the manual's own activity continuum and assessment sections evidence them (flag F8). Read in full in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

**Book 6:** *Activity Based Oral English*, Key Stage 1 (Grades 1 AND 2) — Teacher's Hand Book — National Institute of Education (NIE), 2018 — Sri Lanka. ONE handbook covering both grades (the 2018 replacement for the two old separate guides), filed under both grades as byte-identical assets and cited once (flag F6); teacher-guide evidence. Its Sinhala-medium activity sections (PDF pp.60–73) are the same activities in another medium and are not stocked separately. Read in full in the 2026-11-10 expansion read.

## Papua New Guinea added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Papua New Guinea's in-hand English set from the PDFs — **9 teacher guides, 0 student books**: the 2015 scripted elementary guides for Prep, Grade 1 (E1) and Grade 2 (E2); the 2017 Junior Primary programme guides for Grades 3, 4 and 5; and the Senior Primary programme guides for Grade 6 (2019), Grade 7 (2018) and Grade 8 (2019) — 170 teaching items placed. Every Papua New Guinea item in this section is TEACHER EVIDENCE of what is taught: Papua New Guinea has no student English book in hand, and no row below is pupil-book evidence; teacher guides never substitute for a pupil book. Read depth, as the placements declare it: all nine books read in full, page by page through their text layers (1,546 PDF pages) — in the three elementary guides every daily-lesson page was checked (header, song, phonics step) and the Content/Term Overviews read in full; in the Grades 3–8 programme guides the Content Standards, Yearly Overviews, all strand Weekly Programmes and all sample guided lesson plans were read in full; no book was read at reduced depth. The rows below are stocked from the expansion placements (papua-new-guinea-placements.md); where the placements name the same item for a second lesson, the row appears in that lesson too.

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Canada's in-hand English set from the PDFs — **2 teacher resource guides, 0 student books**: *English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide* (FNESC/FNSA, 2018; 409 pp) and *English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide* (FNESC, 2008; 307 PDF pages), the guides for British Columbia's English First Peoples courses — 145 teaching items placed. Both files were grabbed through the authenticated GitHub route and verified byte-exact against the library record (2018 guide 5,438,437 bytes; 2008 guide 3,393,665 bytes). Every Canada item in this section is TEACHER EVIDENCE of what the BC English First Peoples courses teach: Canada has no student English book in hand, and no row below is pupil-book evidence; a teacher's guide never substitutes for a pupil's book. Read depth, as the placements declare it: both guides read in full, page by page through their text layers (716 PDF pages); the 2018 guide amalgamates and replaces the 2008 guide, so where the two carry the same teaching, each is cited on its own evidence. The rows below are stocked from the expansion placements (canada-placements.md); where the placements name the same item for a second lesson, the row appears in that lesson too.

**Book 2:** *English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher resource guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Canada (British Columbia), First Nations Education Steering Committee (FNESC), ©2008, 307 PDF pages. Printed page = PDF page − 1 (the Table of Contents is printed p.7 = PDF p.8); pages cited are printed pages.

|---|---|
| Planning for Instruction: the power of story — the purposes stories are told for (teaching life lessons and responsibilities, creation stories, recording personal/family/community history, “mapping” geography and resources, cultural continuity, healing, entertainment) and story's forms (prose, song, dance, poetry, theatre, carving, pictures) | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed p.14 |
| First Peoples' Oral Traditions: oral against written story — how a story told from memory differs from a written text; oral tradition, oral history and their relation to the land; the purposes oral traditions serve | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.75–80 |
| Childhood through the Eyes of Indigenous Writers: creating your own children's book on a significant theme for a named young audience, then presenting it to real younger students and taking their response (the audience-choice part is also stocked in choosing-your-register) | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.90–92 |
| First Peoples' Story, Grade 10: the story-word family and story kinds — story against fable and related terms in a word web; the purposes of First Peoples stories revisited as the storyteller's knowledge | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.93–95 |
| First Peoples' Story, Grade 10: building a story from an object — every object has a story: developing a narrative from a chosen everyday or natural object | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.100–101 |
| We Are Our Stories: the narrator's role, and how cultures are reflected in their stories (Thomas King's *The Truth About Stories* chapters as the spine; creation stories as defining a culture's understanding of the world; the spine text is external, not in hand) | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.103–106 |
| We Are Our Stories: rite of passage as a story subject and structure (Jordan Wheeler's “A Mountain Legend”), with written responses to discussion questions | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.108–110 |
| Place-Conscious Learning: setting and place in a First Peoples story — the local stories of the land, important places, and how place shapes what a text means | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.123–130 |
| The Trickster units: the Trickster in traditional stories by nation — Nanabozho stories, Secwepemc Coyote stories, Trickster-as-transformer stories, and local Trickster stories | Canada — English First Peoples, Grades 10–12 — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC/FNSA, 2018) — teacher evidence, printed pp.373–378 |

| Unit 2: The Oral Tradition: teaching narratives — how traditional narratives teach (life lessons), studied from local and non-local First Nations teaching narratives | Canada — English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC, 2008) — teacher evidence, printed pp.39–40 |
| Unit 3: Storytelling: named stories studied with teacher notes, filed at plot level only — Lee Maracle's “Bertha”; Harry Robinson's “Captive in an English Circus” (as told to Wendy Wickwire); Richard Van Camp's “Mermaids”; John McLeod's “The Shivering Tree”; Ruby Slipperjack's “Blueberry Days” (a casual chain of anecdotes about camp life) | Canada — English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC, 2008) — teacher evidence, printed pp.48, 52–61 |
| Unit 9: Identity: the six stories themselves inside the jigsaw teaching — Richard G. Green's “The Last Raven”, Beth Brant's “Swimming Upstream”, Emma Lee Warrior's “Compatriots”, Jordan Wheeler's “A Mountain Legend”, Lee Maracle's “Sojourner's Truth”, Richard Van Camp's “Birthmark”, each with its printed teaching handout (the teaching method itself is stocked in presentation-skills) | Canada — English 12 First Peoples — Teacher Resource Guide (FNESC, 2008) — teacher evidence, printed pp.156–171 |

## Zambia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Zambia's in-hand English set from the PDFs — **12 documents, all teacher-facing: eleven Curriculum Development Centre teaching modules and the Catch Up Literacy Teacher's Guide; 0 pupil's books** — 164 teaching items placed. Every file was secured from its recorded original source (the CDC Digital Library; the Catch Up guide from its recorded VVOB source) at a byte size matching the textbooks repo's contents record exactly, and verified by byte size, page count and cover. Every Zambia item in this section is TEACHER EVIDENCE of what is taught: Zambia has no pupil English book in hand — five of the twelve files are even recorded as “student” books in the records, but their own covers say TEACHING MODULE (expansion flags F2/F3) — and no row below is pupil-book evidence; a teaching module never substitutes for a pupil's book. Two of the modules state why the set looks this way: they were produced “in the absence of approved textbooks.” Read depth, as the placements declare it: all 12 books read in full, page by page through their text layers (the two Grade 1 phonics modules run one seven-part lesson shape per sound — every subtopic's overview and competences were read and the full shape verified in complete reads of multiple subtopics per book; individual exercise lines inside a sound subtopic are practice of the placed sound and are not itemised). The rows below are stocked from the expansion placements (zambia-placements.md); where the placements name the same item for a second lesson, the row appears in that lesson too.

**Book 1:** *Literacy Teacher's Guide — Catch Up Programme (Grades 3–5)* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teacher's guide, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Zambia, Ministry of Education with Pratham, ZESSTA, VVOB, TaRL Africa, UNICEF and USAID, 2023. 35 pp; 7,033,604 bytes, SHA-256 a34cdc66…; grabbed from its recorded VVOB source at exactly its recorded size. Pages cited are the book's printed folios. Language caveat (expansion flag F6): the guide is written in English but directs the teacher to teach in the learners' familiar (Zambian) language and translate all examples, and its sample assessment tool is printed in Cinyanja — its rows are early-literacy SKILLS evidence for Grades 3–5, not English-medium pupil content.

**Book 4:** *Oral English Language Teaching Module, Grade 2 Term 3* (TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY: a teaching module, cited for what it teaches, never as a pupil's book) — Zambia, Curriculum Development Centre, © 2026. 53 pp; 1,259,933 bytes, SHA-256 085f13a4…; CDC Digital Library download byte-matching the textbooks repo's contents record.
